## 2 PRELIMINARIES

## 2.1 YOLO for Astronomical Object Detection

You Onl<sub>y</sub> Look Once (YOLO) is a famil<sub>y</sub> of sin<sub>g</sub>le-sta<sub>g</sub>e dee<sub>p</sub> learning models designed for real-time object detection, first introduced by (Redmon et al. 2016). Unlike two-sta<sub>g</sub>e detectors such as R-CNN and it<sub>s var</sub>i<sub>an</sub>t<sub>s w</sub>hi<sub>c</sub>h fi<sub>rs</sub>t <sub>genera</sub>t<sub>e reg</sub>i<sub>on proposa</sub>l<sub>s an</sub>d <sub>su</sub>b<sub>sequen</sub>tl<sub>y</sub> classify them, YOLO formulates object detection as a unified re-<sub>gress</sub>i<sub>on pro</sub>bl<sub>em.</sub> Gi<sub>ven an</sub> i<sub>npu</sub>t i<sub>mage,</sub> th<sub>e mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l <sub>s</sub>i<sub>mu</sub>lt<sub>aneous</sub>l<sub>y</sub> predicts bounding box coordinates, objectness or confidence scores, <sub>an</sub>d <sub>c</sub>l<sub>ass pro</sub>b<sub>a</sub>biliti<sub>es</sub> i<sub>n a s</sub>i<sub>ng</sub>l<sub>e</sub> f<sub>orwar</sub>d <sub>pass</sub> th<sub>roug</sub>h <sub>a convo</sub>l<sub>u-</sub> tional neural network (CNN).

(ii) Ed<sub>g</sub>e detection usin<sub>g</sub> a Cann<sub>y</sub> filter a<sub>pp</sub>lied to $4 0 " \times 4 0 "$ <sup>i</sup>ma<sub>g</sub>e cutouts

## 3.3 Qualitative Diagnostic Inspection of Baseline Detections

## 4.2 Nature of Residual Detections

Alth<sub>oug</sub>h th<sub>e</sub> fi<sub>na</sub>l <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l <sub>su</sub>b<sub>s</sub>t<sub>an</sub>ti<sub>a</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> <sub>suppresses</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>ar-</sub>i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>uce</sub>d f<sub>a</sub>l<sub>se</sub> <sub>pos</sub>iti<sub>ves re</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> t<sub>o</sub> b<sub>o</sub>th th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>ase</sub>li<sub>ne con</sub>fi<sub>gura</sub>ti<sub>on an</sub>d th<sub>e or</sub>i<sub>g</sub>i<sub>-</sub> <sub>na</sub>l GOTHIC <sub>p</sub>i<sub>pe</sub>li<sub>ne, res</sub>id<sub>ua</sub>l <sub>am</sub>bi<sub>gu</sub>it<sub>y rema</sub>i<sub>ns unavo</sub>id<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> <sub>re</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>ons span severa</sub>l <sub>p</sub>h<sub>ys</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> di<sub>s</sub>ti<sub>nc</sub>t <sub>scenar</sub>i<sub>os:</sub>

(i) Bona fide dual-nucleus s<sub>y</sub>stems consistent with on<sub>g</sub>oin<sub>g</sub> or <sup>late-sta</sup>g<sup>e</sup> g<sup>alax</sup>y <sup>mer</sup>g<sup>ers</sup>.

(ii) A <sub>g</sub>alactic nucleus <sub>p</sub>aired with a fore<sub>g</sub>round star that was not f<sub>u</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> di<sub>sen</sub>t<sub>ang</sub>l<sub>e</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e c</sub>l<sub>ass</sub>ifi<sub>er.</sub>

(iii) A nucleus <sub>p</sub>aired with a faint, low si<sub>g</sub>nal-to-noise, or catalo<sub>g</sub>- <sub>unma</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h<sub>e</sub>d <sub>source.</sub>

## 4.3 Projected Separation Estimation

T<sub>o es</sub>ti<sub>ma</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e p</sub>h<sub>ys</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>l <sub>separa</sub>ti<sub>on o</sub>f d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>nuc</sub>l<sub>ear pa</sub>i<sub>rs, we</sub> adopt a projected-separation approximation. The projected separation is defined as the maximum edge length of the oriented boundin<sub>g</sub> box (OBB) enclosin<sub>g</sub> the detected nuclear <sub>p</sub>air. The full list of these 212 sub-kilo<sub>p</sub>arsec candidates — SDSS objID, coordinates, and redshift — together with their image cutouts, is provided in the Supplementary Material.

Thi<sub>s reg</sub>i<sub>me</sub> i<sub>s o</sub>f <sub>par</sub>ti<sub>cu</sub>l<sub>ar as</sub>t<sub>rop</sub>h<sub>ys</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>l i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>eres</sub>t<sub>: a</sub>t <sub>su</sub>b<sub>-</sub>k<sub>pc sep-</sub> <sub>ara</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>wo nuc</sub>l<sub>e</sub>i <sub>are expec</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d t<sub>o</sub> b<sub>e</sub> d<sub>ynam</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> b<sub>oun</sub>d <sub>an</sub>d <sub>approac</sub>hi<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e grav</sub>it<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ona</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> h<sub>ar</sub>d<sub>ene</sub>d bi<sub>nary s</sub>t<sub>age</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>prece</sub>d<sub>es</sub> coalescence and low-fre uenc ravitational-wave emission (Kharb et al. 2017b). Th<sub>e convers</sub>i<sub>on</sub> t<sub>o p</sub>h<sub>ys</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>l <sub>un</sub>it<sub>s requ</sub>i<sub>res a re</sub>li<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e spec</sub>t<sub>roscop</sub>i<sub>c</sub> <sub>re</sub>d<sub>s</sub>hift f<sub>or</sub> th<sub>e pr</sub>i<sub>mar nuc</sub>l<sub>eus.</sub> Of th<sub>e</sub> 212 <sub>s s</sub>t<sub>ems,</sub> 29 h<sub>ave</sub> $z \ <$ 10<sup>−4</sup><sub>, a va</sub>l<sub>ue sma</sub>ll<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an</sub> th<sub>e genera</sub>l SDSS <sub>re</sub>d<sub>s</sub>hift <sub>uncer</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>y,</sub> <sub>ren</sub>d<sub>er</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> it i<sub>mposs</sub>ibl<sub>e</sub> t<sub>o</sub> di<sub>sam</sub>bi<sub>gua</sub>t<sub>e genu</sub>i<sub>ne compac</sub>t<sub>ness</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> <sub>mere re</sub>d<sub>s</sub>hift <sub>measuremen</sub>t <sub>error.</sub> Th<sub>us, we exc</sub>l<sub>u</sub>d<sub>e</sub> th<sub>ese</sub> 29 <sub>sys</sub>t<sub>ems,</sub> l<sub>eav</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> 183 f<sub>or spec</sub>t<sub>roscop</sub>i<sub>c ana</sub>l<sub>ys</sub>i<sub>s.</sub>

## Method and diagnostic

E<sub>ac</sub>h <sub>o</sub>f th<sub>e</sub> 183 <sub>sys</sub>t<sub>ems was exam</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d <sub>us</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e</sub> SDSS i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erac</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> s<sub>p</sub>ectrum too<sup>l</sup>. $\mathbf { A t } \leq 1$ k<sub>pc</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>wo can</sub>did<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e nuc</sub>l<sub>e</sub>i f<sub>a</sub>ll <sub>w</sub>ithi<sub>n a</sub> sin<sub>g</sub>le SDSS fibre, <sub>y</sub>ieldin<sub>g</sub> one s<sub>p</sub>atiall<sub>y</sub> blended s<sub>p</sub>ectrum (Guo et al. 2012). In this re<sub>g</sub>ime the canonical s<sub>p</sub>ectrosco<sub>p</sub>ic si<sub>g</sub>nature of an unresolved dual active nucleus is a double-peaked narrow emission li<sub>ne,</sub> i<sub>n w</sub>hi<sub>c</sub>h t<sub>wo narrow-</sub>li<sub>ne reg</sub>i<sub>ons, o</sub>f<sub>se</sub>t i<sub>n</sub> li<sub>ne-o</sub>f<sub>-s</sub>i<sub>g</sub>ht <sub>ve</sub>l<sub>oc</sub>it<sub>y,</sub> produce a split or asymmetric profile in lines such as H� and H� (Müller-Sánchez et al. 2015). We accordin<sub>g</sub>l<sub>y</sub> a<sub>pp</sub>lied a two-sta<sub>g</sub>e assessment: 1) whether the s<sub>p</sub>ectrum has suficient si<sub>g</sub>nal-to-noise to be anal<sub>y</sub>sed, 2) whether the Balmer lines show evidence of a double <sub>p</sub>ea<sup>k</sup>.

T<sub>wo</sub> <sub>p</sub>h<sub>ys</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>l <sub>cavea</sub>t<sub>s</sub> <sub>cons</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t th<sub>e</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erpre</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>o</sub>f thi<sub>s</sub> t<sub>es</sub>t<sub>,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d we a<sub>pp</sub><sup>l</sup><sub>y</sub> <sup>i</sup>t conservat<sup>i</sup>ve<sup>l</sup><sub>y</sub>:

First, a velocity splitting is resolvable only if the line-of-sight se<sub>p</sub>aration exceeds the SDSS instrumental resolution (� ∼ 2000, corres ondin to a velocit FWHM of ∼ 150 km s<sup>−1</sup>). For a sub-k c <sub>pa</sub>i<sub>r</sub> th<sub>e or</sub>bit<sub>a</sub>l <sub>ve</sub>l<sub>oc</sub>iti<sub>es</sub> $( \sim 1 0 ^ { 2 } { - } 1 0 ^ { 3 } \ \mathrm { k m } \mathrm { s } ^ { - 1 }$ f<sub>or enc</sub>l<sub>ose</sub>d <sub>masses o</sub>f $\mathsf { \hat { 1 0 } ^ { 1 0 } } \mathrm { - } 1 0 ^ { 1 1 } M _ { \odot } )$ <sub>can</sub> i<sub>n pr</sub>i<sub>nc</sub>i<sub>p</sub>l<sub>e excee</sub>d thi<sub>s</sub> th<sub>res</sub>h<sub>o</sub>ld<sub>,</sub> b<sub>u</sub>t <sub>ran</sub>d<sub>om</sub> projection of the orbital plane reduces the observable line-of-sight <sub>componen</sub>t<sub>, so a</sub> l<sub>arge</sub> f<sub>rac</sub>ti<sub>on o</sub>f <sub>genu</sub>i<sub>ne c</sub>l<sub>ose pa</sub>i<sub>rs wou</sub>ld <sub>presen</sub>t <sub>an unreso</sub>l<sub>ve</sub>d / <sub>marg</sub>i<sub>na</sub>ll b<sub>roa</sub>d<sub>ene</sub>d li<sub>ne.</sub> A <sub>non-</sub>d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on o</sub>f <sub>a</sub> d<sub>ou</sub>bl<sub>e ea</sub>k th<sub>ere</sub>f<sub>ore</sub> d<sub>oes no</sub>t <sub>a exc</sub>l<sub>u</sub>d<sub>e enu</sub>i<sub>ne</sub> d<sub>ua</sub>l <sub>nuc</sub>l<sub>eus.</sub>

Second, double- eaked narrow lines are an im ure tracer: rotatin gas discs, biconical AGN outflows, and jet–ISM interactions can all <sub>ro</sub>d<sub>uce</sub> d<sub>ou</sub>bl<sub>e- ea</sub>k<sub>e</sub>d <sub>ro</sub>fil<sub>es</sub> i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e a</sub>b<sub>sence o</sub>f <sub>a secon</sub>d <sub>nuc</sub>l<sub>eus</sub> (Müller-Sánchez et al. 2015; Kharb et al. 2015, 2021). ## Interpretation

Th<sub>e spec</sub>t<sub>roscop</sub>i<sub>c resu</sub>lt<sub>s y</sub>i<sub>e</sub>ld <sub>no con</sub>fi<sub>rme</sub>d d<sub>ou</sub>bl<sub>e-pea</sub>k<sub>e</sub>d emission-line dual-nucleus candidate within the ≤ 1 k<sub>p</sub>c subset. We <sub>s</sub>t<sub>ress</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t thi<sub>s nega</sub>ti<sub>ve resu</sub>lt <sub>nee</sub>d<sub>s no</sub>t <sub>necessar</sub>il<sub>y</sub> b<sub>e</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erpre</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>as</sub> f<sub>a</sub>il<sub>ure,</sub> b<sub>u</sub>t i<sub>s ra</sub>th<sub>er expec</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d d<sub>ue</sub> t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>o</sub>ll<sub>ow</sub>i<sub>ng reasons :</sub>

(i) Single-fibre blending removes spatial information at these se<sub>p</sub>arat<sup>i</sup>ons.

(ii) The SDSS spectral resolution, combined with projection, renders th<sub>e ve</sub>l<sub>oc</sub>it<sub>y sp</sub>lit <sub>o</sub>f <sub>a genu</sub>i<sub>ne su</sub>b<sub>-</sub>k<sub>pc pa</sub>i<sub>r</sub> f<sub>requen</sub>tl<sub>y uno</sub>b<sub>serva</sub>bl<sub>e.</sub> (iii) Double-peaked profiles are in any case an ambiguous tracer.

## 5 DISCUSSION

## APPENDIX A: YOLO ARCHITECTURE

YOLO (You Onl Look Once) formulates object detection as a sin le-<sub>s</sub>t<sub>age,</sub> d<sub>ense pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>on pro</sub>bl<sub>em</sub> i<sub>n w</sub>hi<sub>c</sub>h l<sub>oca</sub>li<sub>za</sub>ti<sub>on an</sub>d <sub>c</sub>l<sub>ass</sub>ifi<sub>ca-</sub> tion are jointly optimized within a single forward pass Redmon et al. (2016).

## A1 Network Formulation

L<sub>e</sub>t <sub>an</sub> i<sub>npu</sub>t i<sub>mage</sub> b<sub>e</sub> d<sub>eno</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d b<sub>y</sub>

$$
\mathbf { I } \in \mathbb { R } ^ { H \times W \times 3 } .\tag{A1}
$$

Th<sub>e</sub> YOLO n<sub>e</sub>t<sub>wo</sub>rk r<sub>ep</sub>r<sub>ese</sub>nt<sub>s</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>pa</sub>r<sub>a</sub>m<sub>e</sub>tri<sub>c</sub> m<sub>app</sub>in<sub>g</sub>

$$
f _ { \theta } : \mathbf { I }  \mathbf { Y } ,\tag{A2}
$$

where � denotes the learnable parameters and Y is the multi-scale detection tensor containing bounding box parameters, objectness <sub>scores, an</sub>d <sub>c</sub>l<sub>ass ro</sub>b<sub>a</sub>biliti<sub>es.</sub>

M<sub>o</sub>d<sub>ern</sub> YOLO <sub>arc</sub>hit<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>ures</sub> <sub>cons</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t <sub>o</sub>f th<sub>ree</sub> <sub>pr</sub>i<sub>nc</sub>i<sub>pa</sub>l <sub>compo-</sub> nents:

(i) Backbone: A fully convolutional feature extractor (CSP/C3- st<sub>y</sub>le blocks in YOLOv11) that <sub>g</sub>enerates hierarchical feature ma<sub>p</sub>s <sub>a</sub>t <sub>mu</sub>lti<sub>p</sub>l<sub>e spa</sub>ti<sub>a</sub>l <sub>reso</sub>l<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>ons.</sub>

(ii) Neck: A feature pyramid and path-aggregation network (FPN/PAN) that fuses low-level s<sub>p</sub>atial information with hi<sub>g</sub>h-level <sub>seman</sub>ti<sub>c</sub> f<sub>ea</sub>t<sub>ures.</sub>

(iii) Detection Head: A set of rediction la ers o eratin at multiple scales, each regressing bounding box geometry, objectness, and <sub>c</sub>l<sub>ass pro</sub>b<sub>a</sub>biliti<sub>es.</sub>

F<sub>or eac</sub>h <sub>spa</sub>ti<sub>a</sub>l l<sub>oca</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> i<sub>n a</sub> f<sub>ea</sub>t<sub>ure map,</sub> th<sub>e</sub> d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>or pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>t<sub>s:</sub>

$$
\hat { \mathbf { y } } = ( \hat { \mathbf { b } } , \hat { o } , \hat { \mathbf { p } } )\tag{A3}
$$

<sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere:</sub>

• $\hat { \mathbf { b } }$ <sub>represen</sub>t<sub>s</sub> b<sub>oun</sub>di<sub>ng</sub> b<sub>ox parame</sub>t<sub>ers,</sub>

$\hat { \mathbf p } \in [ 0 , 1 ] ^ { C }$ i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e vec</sub>t<sub>or o</sub>f <sub>c</sub>l<sub>ass pro</sub>b<sub>a</sub>biliti<sub>es</sub> f<sub>or</sub> � <sub>c</sub>l<sub>asses.</sub>

## A2 Oriented Bounding Box (OBB) Parameterization

I<sub>n s</sub>t<sub>an</sub>d<sub>ar</sub>d YOLO <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s,</sub> b<sub>oun</sub>di<sub>ng</sub> b<sub>oxes are ax</sub>i<sub>s-a</sub>li<sub>gne</sub>d <sub>an</sub>d <sub>parame</sub>t<sub>er</sub>i<sub>ze</sub>d <sub>as</sub> $( x , y , w , h )$ <sub>.</sub> I<sub>n con</sub>t<sub>ras</sub>t<sub>,</sub> YOLO<sub>v</sub>11<sub>–</sub>OBB <sub>ex</sub>t<sub>en</sub>d<sub>s</sub> this formulation to explicitly model object rotation.

Each object is represented by four ordered corner points:

$$
\mathbf { b } = ( x _ { 1 } , y _ { 1 } , x _ { 2 } , y _ { 2 } , x _ { 3 } , y _ { 3 } , x _ { 4 } , y _ { 4 } ) ,\tag{A4}
$$

<sub>w</sub>ith $( x _ { i } , y _ { i } ) ~ \in ~ [ 0 , 1 ]$ d<sub>eno</sub>ti<sub>ng norma</sub>li<sub>ze</sub>d i<sub>mage coor</sub>di<sub>na</sub>t<sub>es.</sub> Thi<sub>s represen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on a</sub>ll<sub>ows</sub> th<sub>e mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l t<sub>o</sub> l<sub>earn ar</sub>bit<sub>rary qua</sub>d<sub>r</sub>il<sub>a</sub>t<sub>era</sub>l <sub>geome</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>es, cap</sub>t<sub>ur</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> i<sub>nc</sub>li<sub>ne</sub>d<sub>, e</sub>l<sub>onga</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d<sub>, or asymme</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c nuc</sub>l<sub>ear</sub> structures.

F<sub>orma</sub>ll<sub>y,</sub> th<sub>e regress</sub>i<sub>on</sub> t<sub>as</sub>k b<sub>ecomes:</sub>

$$
{ \hat { \mathbf { b } } } = f _ { \theta } ( \mathbf { I } ) ,\tag{A5}
$$

<sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> $\hat { \mathbf { b } } \in \mathbb { R } ^ { 8 }$ <sub>pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>t<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e ro</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d b<sub>oun</sub>di<sub>ng</sub> b<sub>ox coor</sub>di<sub>na</sub>t<sub>es.</sub>

Thi<sub>s parame</sub>t<sub>er</sub>i<sub>za</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> i<sub>s par</sub>ti<sub>cu</sub>l<sub>ar</sub>l<sub>y a</sub>d<sub>van</sub>t<sub>ageous</sub> f<sub>or as</sub>t<sub>ronom</sub>i<sub>-</sub> <sub>ca</sub>l i<sub>mag</sub>i<sub>ng, w</sub>h<sub>ere ga</sub>l<sub>axy mergers ex</sub>hibit <sub>no pre</sub>f<sub>erre</sub>d <sub>or</sub>i<sub>en</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> on the sky and are subject to projection efects.

## A3 Loss Function

The training objective minimizes a composite detection loss:

$$
\mathcal { L } _ { t o t a l } = \lambda _ { b o x } \mathcal { L } _ { b o x } + \lambda _ { c l s } \mathcal { L } _ { c l s } + \lambda _ { o b j } \mathcal { L } _ { o b j } ,\tag{A6}
$$

<sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere:</sub>

$\mathcal { L } _ { b o x }$ is an IoU-based re<sub>g</sub>ression loss (e.<sub>g</sub>., GIoU/DIoU) bet<sub>ween pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>an</sub>d <sub>groun</sub>d<sub>-</sub>t<sub>ru</sub>th OBB<sub>s,</sub>

$\mathcal { L } _ { c l s }$ i<sub>s a</sub> bi<sub>nary cross-en</sub>t<sub>ropy</sub> l<sub>oss over c</sub>l<sub>ass pro</sub>b<sub>a</sub>biliti<sub>es,</sub>

$\mathcal { L } _ { o b j }$ is the objectness confidence loss.

F<sub>o</sub>r <sub>a</sub>n I<sub>o</sub>U<sub>-</sub>b<sub>ase</sub>d r<sub>eg</sub>r<sub>ess</sub>i<sub>o</sub>n t<sub>e</sub>rm<sub>:</sub>

$$
\mathcal { L } _ { b o x } = 1 - \mathrm { I o U } ( \hat { \mathbf { b } } , \mathbf { b } ) ,\tag{A7}
$$

<sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> I<sub>o</sub>U i<sub>s compu</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>over or</sub>i<sub>en</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>po</sub>l<sub>ygona</sub>l <sub>reg</sub>i<sub>ons.</sub>

Th<sub>e ne</sub>t<sub>wor</sub>k <sub>parame</sub>t<sub>ers are op</sub>ti<sub>m</sub>i<sub>ze</sub>d <sub>v</sub>i<sub>a s</sub>t<sub>oc</sub>h<sub>as</sub>ti<sub>c gra</sub>di<sub>en</sub>t d<sub>e-</sub> scent:

$$
\theta _ { t + 1 } = \theta _ { t } - \eta \nabla _ { \theta } \mathcal { L } _ { t o t a l } ,\tag{A8}
$$

<sub>w</sub>ith l<sub>earn</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>ra</sub>t<sub>e</sub> <sub>�.</sub>

Fi<sub>gure</sub> A1 ill<sub>us</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>t<sub>es</sub> th<sub>e</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erna</sub>l t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> l<sub>oop execu</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>a</sub>t <sub>eac</sub>h <sub>epoc</sub>h<sub>.</sub> D<sub>ur</sub>i<sub>ng eac</sub>h it<sub>era</sub>ti<sub>on:</sub>

(ii) A forward <sub>p</sub>ass <sub>p</sub>redicts boundin<sub>g</sub> boxes, objectness scores, <sub>an</sub>d <sub>c</sub>l<sub>ass pro</sub>b<sub>a</sub>biliti<sub>es.</sub>

Aft<sub>er eac</sub>h <sub>epoc</sub>h<sub>,</sub> th<sub>e mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l i<sub>s eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>on</sub> th<sub>e va</sub>lid<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on se</sub>t without <sub>g</sub>radient u<sub>p</sub>dates. Performance metrics (Precision, Recall, F<sub>1</sub>-score, mAP, and related dia<sub>g</sub>nostics) are recorded. ## APPENDIX D: PROJECTED SEPARATION ESTIMATION ALGORITHM

A key objective of this study is to quantify the minimum projected <sub>p</sub>h<sub>ys</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>l <sub>separa</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> b<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ween</sub> d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d d<sub>ua</sub>l<sub>-nuc</sub>l<sub>eus sys</sub>t<sub>ems.</sub> Di<sub>rec</sub>t <sub>es</sub>ti<sub>ma</sub>ti<sub>on o</sub>f th<sub>ree-</sub>di<sub>mens</sub>i<sub>ona</sub>l <sub>separa</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> i<sub>s no</sub>t f<sub>eas</sub>ibl<sub>e</sub> f<sub>or</sub> th<sub>e</sub> majority of detections due to the absence of spectroscopic redshifts f<sub>or</sub> b<sub>o</sub>th <sub>componen</sub>t<sub>s.</sub> W<sub>e</sub> th<sub>ere</sub>f<sub>ore a</sub>d<sub>op</sub>t <sub>a p</sub>h<sub>ys</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>ll<sub>y mo</sub>ti<sub>va</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d approximation to estimate projected separations using image-plane <sub>geome</sub>t<sub>ry com</sub>bi<sub>ne</sub>d <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>ava</sub>il<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e spec</sub>t<sub>roscop</sub>i<sub>c</sub> i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>orma</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub>

For each <sub>p</sub>ost-filtered detection labelled as a dual nucleus (Class 0), th<sub>e</sub> YOLO<sub>v</sub>11<sub>x-</sub>OBB <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l <sub>ou</sub>t<sub>pu</sub>t<sub>s an or</sub>i<sub>en</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d b<sub>oun</sub>di<sub>ng</sub> b<sub>ox</sub> (OBB) enclosin<sub>g</sub> both nuclear com<sub>p</sub>onents. The OBB is re<sub>p</sub>resented b<sub>y</sub> f<sub>our norma</sub>li<sub>se</sub>d <sub>corner coor</sub>di<sub>na</sub>t<sub>es</sub> i<sub>n</sub> i<sub>mage space:</sub>

$$
( x _ { 1 } , y _ { 1 } ) , ( x _ { 2 } , y _ { 2 } ) , ( x _ { 3 } , y _ { 3 } ) , ( x _ { 4 } , y _ { 4 } ) ,\tag{D1}

Let � ∼ Binomia $\left[ \left( n , p \right) \right.$ d<sub>eno</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e num</sub>b<sub>er</sub> <sub>o</sub>f t<sub>rue</sub> d<sub>ua</sub>l<sub>-nuc</sub>l<sub>eus sys</sub>t<sub>ems</sub> id<sub>en</sub>tifi<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n a samp</sub>l<sub>e o</sub>f <sub>s</sub>i<sub>ze �, w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> <sub>�</sub> i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e un</sub>d<sub>er</sub>l<sub>y</sub>i<sub>ng pro</sub>b<sub>a</sub>bilit<sub>y</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>a ran</sub>d<sub>om</sub>l<sub>y se</sub>l<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>correspon</sub>d<sub>s</sub> t<sub>o a genu</sub>i<sub>ne</sub> d<sub>ua</sub>l <sub>nuc</sub>l<sub>eus.</sub> Th<sub>e emp</sub>i<sub>r</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>l <sub>es</sub>ti<sub>ma</sub>t<sub>or</sub> f<sub>or</sub> thi<sub>s pro</sub>b<sub>a</sub>bilit<sub>y</sub> i<sub>s:</sub>

$$

$$

<sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> � i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e num</sub>b<sub>er o</sub>f d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>ons c</sub>l<sub>ass</sub>ifi<sub>e</sub>d <sub>as</sub> d<sub>ua</sub>l <sub>nuc</sub>l<sub>e</sub>i<sub>.</sub> F<sub>or su</sub>f<sub>-</sub> fi<sub>c</sub>i<sub>en</sub>tl<sub>y</sub> l<sub>arge �,</sub> th<sub>e samp</sub>li<sub>ng</sub> di<sub>s</sub>t<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>on o</sub>f $\hat { p }$ <sub>can</sub> b<sub>e approx</sub>i<sub>ma</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>as norma</sub>l <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>var</sub>i<sub>ance:</sub>

## THE SUB-KILOPARSEC CANDIDATE SAMPLE

This supplement lists and illustrates the most compact dual-nucleus candidates identified in this work: the 212 YOLOv11x–OBB detections whose GOTHIC-calibrated centroid-to-centroid separation satisfies � ≤ 1 kpc, i.e. the leftmost bin of the projected physical-separation distribution (Section 4.7 of the main paper). At sub-kiloparsec separations the two nuclei are unresolved in SDSS imaging, so these are dual-nucleus candidates rather than confirmed dual AGN. Table 1 gives their SDSS objID, coordinates and redshift, and Fig. 1 shows the corresponding image cutouts; each objID in the table hyperlinks to its cutout.
